Let's design local search more carefully.
Fast score function can compute convolution coefficients via FFT. For n up to maybe 16384, N=32768, FFT ~0.02s. 2400s => ~5000 scores. That's enough if each local iteration one score. But multiplicative update needs active coefficients; we need coefficients from last score. We can cache.
Algorithm for a candidate p:
  b, score = conv_score(p)
  top_indices = argpartition(b, -m) for m maybe 8 or 16.
  For each active k, update weights: grad[i] += p[j] where j=k-i. But for all top m, use maybe weighted by violation: w_k = max(0, b_k - (1-δ)max_b). This emphasizes peaks.
  Then p_new = p * exp(-eta*grad) (or divide by (1+eta*grad)) to avoid underflow. Normalize sum=1.
  Optionally reflect/random.
This is like gradient descent on sum of top violations. But if top coefficients include nearly all, update may smooth. Need choose eta adapt. Score after update may increase. We can accept if new_score < best_local or with small probability; else revert. We can also do a backtracking line search: try several eta values (e.g., 0.001..1) and choose best.
Because p length n and top m, computing grad O(mn). If n=16k, m=8, ~128k ops per iter, okay.
Potential issue: Multiplicative update with grad partner weights can make some p extremely small/large, but nonneg. Renormalization. Could converge to zeros/sinks. Add small floor? We can periodically mix with best to avoid degeneracy.
Also use simulated annealing: occasionally apply block multiplicative noise and accept if score<current or probabilistically. This can escape local minima.
We can alternate between "peak suppression" gradient and "noise".

Candidate evaluation fast:
```
def fast_conv_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 1024:
        b = np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
        N=1
        while N < 2*n-1: N <<=1
        F = np.fft.fft(a, N)
        b = np.fft.ifft(F*F).real[:2*n-1]
        b = np.where(b<0,0,b)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <=1e-12: return inf, b
    maxb = b.max()
    score = 2*n*maxb/(s*s)
    return score, b
```
Because scale invariant, we can normalize a sum1 after each update to keep numbers small. fast_score assumes nonnormalized? It uses sum. Good.

Local peak suppression:
```
def peak_update(p, b, eta, top_count, noise=0.0, rng=None):
    n=p.size
    idx = np.argpartition(b, -top_count)[-top_count:]
    # weights by exceedance
    vmax = b[idx].max()
    w = np.zeros(n)
    for k in idx:
       wk = 0.5*(b[k]/vmax)  or max(0,b[k]-0.9vmax)
       i0=max(0,k-(n-1)); i1=min(n-1,k)
       j0=k-i1; j1=k-i0
       w[i0:i1+1] += wk * p[j0:j1+1][::-1]
    p2 = p * np.exp(-eta*w)
    s=p2.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return p
    p2 /= s
    if noise>0: multiply random block? 
    return p2
```
This update decreases p_i where partner weights in peaks are high. Maybe should add a baseline to keep total? Renorm. If p has zeros, fine.
We can also use the full gradient of sum of active with weight = log(b_k/vmax)? But okay.

Using grad=p_j gives ∂/∂p_i =p_j; update p_i *= exp(-η p_j) decreases p_i. Product decreases by factor exp(-η p_j). If p_j also decreases, product decreases more. Good.
Maybe better update p_i *= exp(-η p_i p_j) to penalize product directly. But grad for objective q is p_j, not p_i p_j for multiplicative? If change log p_i, derivative 2p_i p_j. So use grad_log = p_j * p_i? We can multiply by p_i for log update. Let's choose log update: p_i ← p_i * exp(-2η ∑ active p_{k-i} p_i) = p_i exp(-2η p_i partner). For constant, gradient p_i^2 same all, no change. For random, penalizes high product directly. But if p_i small, small log change; if p_i large and partner moderate, larger. def local_search(p0, max_time, rng, global_best):
    current = p0.copy(); score = fast_score
    best = current; best_score=score
    eta = 0.1
    T = 0.05
    iter=0
    while time < limit:
       # print progress every 2s
       # choose action
       if rng.random()<0.75:
           score,b = fast_conv_score(current) # maybe already have b; store b to avoid recompute? After update need new score anyway.
           new = peak_update(current,b,eta,top_count, ...)
       else:
           new = perturb(current,rng)
       new_score,_ = fast_conv_score(new)
       if finite and (new_score <= current*1.0 + ...): accept
       # occasionally (every 50) mix with best to avoid drift: current = mix(current,best,0.2)
       # adjust eta/T based on acceptance
       if new_score < best_score:
           best = new.copy(); best_score=...
           update global
       iter++
```

Let's refine peak_update to be efficient:
```
def peak_update(p, b, eta, top_count, rng):
    n = len(p)
    if top_count <=0: return p
    if top_count >= len(b): idx = np.arange(len(b))
    else: idx = np.argpartition(b, -top_count)[-top_count:]
    # weight peaks by relative amount above second? Use b[idx]
    # if all b positive
    vmax = b[idx].max()
    if vmax <=0: return p
    w = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in idx:
        wk = b[k] / vmax  # 1 for max, lower for others
        i0 = max(0, k-(n-1))
        i1 = min(n-1, k)
        if i0 > i1: continue
        # partner indices: j = k - i, descending when i ascending
        j0 = k - i1
        j1 = k - i0
        # p[j0:j1+1] reversed length i1-i0+1
        w[i0:i1+1] += wk * p[j0:j1+1][::-1]
    # Ensure no zeros; maybe add small epsilon * mean
    min_p = np.finfo(np.float64).tiny
    # Multiplicative update: p * exp(-eta*w/wmean) to keep step scale
    scale = w.mean()
    if scale > 0:
        updates = np.exp(-eta * (w / scale))
    else:
        updates = np.ones(n)
    p2 = p * updates
    # add tiny floor to allow recovery from zeros? but if p had zeros, p2 zeros. Add eps*max(p)
    p2 += 1e-12 * max(1e-12, p.max())
    s=p2.sum()
    if not finite: return p
    p2 /= s
    return p2
```
Using log update with w = partner. If we add floor, zeros not fully recovered. Could instead sample small random positive every update: p2 += 1e-9/n; normalize. This keeps all positive. But zeros may be useful; 1e-9 negligible.
```
p2 = p*cos? + noise? 
```
Maybe use p2 = (1-α)*p*updates + α*uniform, α=1e-4 to keep positivity and explore. But this adds entropy and may prevent convergence. Use α=1e-6.
If p has very small values, log may underflow. Multiplicative okay.

Alternative update: `p2 = p * (1 + eta * (1 - normalized_w))`? Not.

def perturb(p, rng, mode):
   q=p.copy()
   # choose blocks
   nb = rng.integers(1,4)
   for _ in range(nb):
      width = int(n * np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.5))) maybe min1 max n
      start = rng.integers(0,n-width+1)
      q[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma))
   # occasionally reassign a block from uniform random
   if rng.random()<0.2:
       width = ...; q[start:start+width] /= q[start:start+width].sum(); q[start:start+width] *= q[start:start+width].sum()?? need renormalize.
   normalize and clip.
```

```
def make_power_seq(n, p, rev=False, eps=1e-3):
   t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   a=(t+eps)**p
   if rev: a=a[::-1]
   return normalize(a)
```
But p can be negative causing inf at0; use eps.
`make_beta_seq(n,p,q,eps=1e-4)`.
`make_double_exp_seq(n, lam, mu, s)`: a = exp(lam*t) + s*exp(mu*(1-t)); if lam/mu large under/overflow; subtract max.
`make_quadratic_rep_seq(n,A,B)`: a=exp(A*t^2+B*t); subtract max for stability? If A positive large, endpoint high; okay.
`make_cos_seq(n, coeffs, phases)`: a=1+sum coeff_k cos(2π k t + phase), clip to 0, if all zero uniform. coeff range maybe [-1,1]. Ensure nonnegative by adding offset: a=1+0.8*sum...; clip.

Normalize function:
```
def normalize(a):
  a=np.asarray(a,float)
  a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0); a=np.maximum(a,0)
  s=a.sum()
  if s<=1e-15: return np.full_like(a,1/a.size)
  return a/s
```

Initial list for a length:
```
def generate_initials(n, rng, time_limit=None, state_evals=50):
  inits=[]
  # constant
  inits.append(normalize(np.ones(n)))

def fast_conv(a):
 n=a.size
 if n <= DIRECT_N:
    b=np.convolve(a,a)
 else:
    N=1; req=2*n-1
    while N<req: N<<=1
    fa=np.fft.fft(a,N)
    b=np.fft.ifft(fa*fa).real[:req]
    # due to error, also enforce nonnegative but not too aggressive: b=np.maximum(b,0)
 return b
```

7. `perturb_normalized(p, rng, sigma=0.2)`:
```
 q=p.astype(float).copy()
 n=p.size
 nblocks = int(rng.integers(1,4))
 for _ in range(nblocks):
   width = int(np.clip(n * np.exp(rng.normal(-3.0,1.0)), 1, n)) # mostly small
   start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1)) if width<n else 0
   factor = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma))
   q[start:start+width] *= factor
 # sometimes zero-ish a block
 if rng.random()<0.15:
   width=...; q[start:start+width] *= 0.2
 # add tiny positive and renormalize
 q += 1e-12
 s=q.sum(); if not finite or s<=0: return p
 return q/s
```
`sigma` maybe adaptive.
8. `crossover(p1,p2,rng,use_geom=False)`:

 else:

```

Pseudo:
```
def local_search(parent, deadline, rng, state, step_names):
    p = parent.copy(); score,b = conv_score_b(p)
    best = p.copy(); best_score=score
    update_state_if_better(best,best_score)
    T=5e-4; eta=0.2; since=0
    iter=0
    parent_start=time
    time_per_iter estimate? no
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # if p far from best (score > best*1.05) and time for parent >5s, reset to best with perturb
       if best_score < score*0.95 and iter>50:
          p = best.copy(); score=best_score
       # action
       r = rng.random()
       if r < 0.65:
          # peak update
          # need b from current score; if we have b cache from last eval? Use b.
          new = peak_update(p, b, eta, top_count, rng)
       elif r < 0.8:
          new = perturb_normalized(p, rng, sigma)
       elif r < 0.9:
          # crossover with state top? Could use parent and best
          new = crossover(p, best, rng)
       else:
          new = perturb_normalized(parent, rng, sigma*0.5)
       new_score, new_b = conv_score_b(new)
       if not finite:
          new=p; new_score=score; new_b=b; iter++; continue
       # acceptance
       if new_score < score or (new_score <= score*1.002 and rng.random() < np.exp(-(new_score-score)/T)):
          p=new.copy(); score=b? ; b=new_b
          # cool
          T = max(1e-7, T*0.999)
          eta = max(0.01, min(1.0, eta*0.999?))
          # adapt eta: if improved often, increase? maybe.
       else:
          # if rejected and many, reduce step/noise
          since +=1
          if since > 50:
             T*=0.5; eta*=0.7; since=0
             # drift toward best
             p = 0.8*p +0.2*best; p=normalize
             score,b=conv_score_b(p)
       iter+=1
       if new_score < best_score:
          best = p.copy(); best_score=score
          update_state
       # print occasionally
       if iter % 200 ==0: print progress maybe with n and score
    return best,best_score
```
Need top_count: `max(4, min(64, n//32))`? For n=16 top 4; for n=16384 top64. Use 8? Top 32 maybe more active. For constant, top_count 32 helps break. For n large, peak_update O(32*n) okay. Choose `top_count = max(4, min(32, n//16))`? For n=1024:32; 4096:32. Good.
Eta: initial 0.5? Multiplicative factor p*exp(-eta*w/scale). w scale mean. If eta too large, destructive. Use eta=0.2, maybe anneal up/down. We can update eta = 0.9*eta +0.1? Better: keep small 0.1-0.3. For peak update to take effect, eta maybe 0.5. Let's use eta=0.3, if many rejections reduce to 0.1.
Temperature for acceptance: score difference small. T=1e-4. exp(-(Δ)/T) may underflow for Δ>0.01. Good.
Maybe use relative acceptance: accept if new_score < score or (new_score/score < 1+T). Simplify:
```
if new_score < score:
   accept
else:
   ratio = new_score/score
   if ratio < 1+T: accept with prob exp(-(ratio-1)/T)
```

Top functions:
- `def norm_tol(a):`
```
def safe_normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0])
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum(dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.shape, 1.0 / a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s
```

- `def next_pow2(x):`

- `def fast_convolution(p):`
```
def fast_convolution(p):
    n = p.size
    req = 2*n -1
    if n <= 768:
       b = np.convolve(p,p)
       return b
    N=1
    while N < req: N <<=1
    fa = np.fft.fft(p, N)
    b = np.fft.ifft(fa * fa)
    b = b.real[:req]
    # Remove tiny negatives
    # Use where; if some large negative due numerical? clip.
    return np.maximum(b, 0.0)
```
- `def conv_score_b(p):`
```
def conv_score_b(p):
   p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
   n = p.size
   if n ==0: return (float('inf'), None)
   s = p.sum(dtype=np.float64)
   if not finite or s<=1e-300: return (inf, None)
   p = p/s
   b = fast_convolution(p)
   m = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
   if not finite(m): return inf,b
   return (2.0*n*m, b)
```

- `def exact_evaluate(seq):`
```
def exact_evaluate(seq):
    try:
       val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in seq])
       if np.isfinite(val): return float(val)
    except Exception:
       pass
    return float('inf')
```

- `def item_score(x): return x[0]`
- `def item_seq(x): return x[1]`

Initial seq functions:
```
def seq_constant(n): return safe_normalize(np.ones(n))

def seq_power(n, exponent, reverse=False, eps=1e-6):
    t=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    a=np.power(t+eps, exponent)
    if reverse: a=a[::-1]
    return safe_normalize(a)

def seq_beta(n,p,q,eps=1e-6):
    t=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    a=np.power(t+eps,p)*np.power(1.0-t+eps,q)
    return safe_normalize(a)

def seq_exp_linear(n, c0, c1, reverse=False): # a=exp(c0 t + c1 (1-t))
   t=...
   a=np.exp(c0*t + c1*(1-t))

def seq_double_exp(n, c0, c1, s):
    t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    e1=np.exp(c0*t)
    e2=s*np.exp(c1*(1-t))
    a=e1+e2
    # if overflow? c up to 20 no; but if c negative large underflow fine
    return safe_normalize(a)
```

   a += strength*coeffs[k-1]*np.cos(2*np.pi*k*t + phases[k-1])

def state_update(state, seq, score):
    if not np.isfinite(score): return
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if score < state['score']:
        state['score']=score
        state['seq']=seq.copy()
    by=state.get('by_n')
    if by is None:
        by={}; state['by_n']=by
    n=seq.size
    old=by.get(n)
    if old is None or score < old[0]:
        by[n]=[score, seq.copy()]

Let's consider potential bug with `np.argpartition(b, -top_count)` when top_count equals len(b) or >. Condition: if top_count >= b.size, idx=np.arange(b.size); else argpartition. For b size 2n-1 maybe small.

`peak_update` with top_count maybe > b.size if n=2. okay.
```
def peak_update(p,b,eta,top_count):
    n=p.size
    if b is None or b.size==0: return p.copy()
    m = len(b)
    if top_count <=1: top_count=1
    if top_count >= m:
       idx=np.arange(m)
    else:
       idx=np.argpartition(b, m-top_count)[m-top_count:]
    vmax = float(b[idx].max())
    if vmax <=0: return p.copy()
    w=np.zeros(n)
    for k in idx:
       wk=(float(b[k])/vmax)**2
       if wk <=0: continue
       i0 = k-(n-1)
       if i0<0: i0=0
       i1 = k
       if i1>n-1: i1=n-1
       if i0>i1: continue
       j0 = k - i1
       j1 = k - i0
       # partner slice j0..j1 length equals i1-i0+1
       # p[j0:j1+1][::-1]
       w[i0:i1+1] += wk * p[j0:j1+1][::-1]
    if w.sum() <=0: return p.copy()
    w /= w.mean()
    alpha=1e-6
    up = (1-alpha)*np.exp(-eta*w) + alpha
    p2 = p*up
    p2 += 1e-12*np.maximum(1.0, p.max())
    s=p2.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: return p.copy()
    return p2/s
```
If p has zeros, p2 zeros + 1e-12, so slight recovery. Good.

`perturb_normalized`:
```
def perturb_normalized(p, rng, sigma=0.25):
    n=p.size
    q=p.astype(float, copy=True)
    nb = int(rng.integers(1,3))
    for _ in range(nb):
       logw = rng.normal(-3.0,1.2)
       width = int(np.exp(logw) * n)
       width = max(1, min(n, width))
       if width <=0: width=1
       start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1)) if n>width else 0
       q[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
    if rng.random() < 0.15:
       width = int(min(n, max(1, n*rng.uniform(0.01,0.2))))
       start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1)) if n>width else 0
       q[start:start+width] *= 0.25
    q = np.where(np.isfinite(q), q, 0.0)
    q = np.maximum(q,0.0)
    q += 1e-12
    s=q.sum()
    if not finite or s<=1e-300: return p.copy()
    return q/s
```
Note `rng.integers(0, n-width+1)` requires high > low; if n-width+1=1, returns 0; okay if n>=width.

`mix_normalized`:
```
def mix_normalized(p1,p2,rng):
    n=p1.size
    if rng.random()<0.5:
       q=np.sqrt(np.maximum(p1,0)*np.maximum(p2,0))
    else:
       w=float(rng.uniform(0.15,0.85))
       q=w*p1+(1-w)*p2
    q=np.where(np.isfinite(q),q,0); q=np.maximum(q,0); q +=1e-12
    s=q.sum(); return q/s if s>0 else p1.copy()
```

`local_search` details with no clocks? Use time.
```
def local_search(parent, deadline, rng, state, verbose=True):
    p = np.asarray(parent, float).copy()
    score,b = conv_score_b(p)
    if not finite: return
    best = p.copy(); best_score=score
    state_update(state,best,best_score)
    start=time.monotonic()
    T=1e-4; eta=0.35; top_count=max(4, min(32, max(1,p.size//16)))
    rejected=0; iter=0
    last_print=start
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if best_score < score * 0.98 and rejected > 100:
            p=best.copy(); score=best_score
        r=rng.random()
        if r < 0.70 and b is not None:
            new=peak_update(p,b,eta,top_count)
        elif r < 0.85:
            new=perturb_normalized(p,rng,sigma=0.2)
        elif r < 0.95:
            new=mix_normalized(p,best,rng)
        else:
            new=perturb_normalized(p,rng,sigma=0.4)
        new_score,new_b=conv_score_b(new)
        if not np.isfinite(new_score):
            continue
        if new_score < score:
            p=new.copy(); b=new_b; score=new_score; rejected=0; T=max(1e-7,T*0.995)
        else:
            ratio=new_score/score
            accept=False
            if ratio < 1.0 + T:
                if rng.random() < float(np.exp(-(ratio-1.0)/T)):
                    accept=True
            if accept:
                p=new.copy(); b=new_b; score=new_score
            else:
                rejected +=1
                if rejected > 80:
                    rejected=0
                    T=max(1e-7, T*0.5)
                    eta=max(0.05, eta*0.75)
                    p = 0.8*p + 0.2*best
                    s=p.sum(); p/=s if s>0
                    score,b=conv_score_b(p)
        if score < best_score:
            best=p.copy(); best_score=score
            state_update(state,best,best_score)
        iter +=1
        now=time.monotonic()
        if now - last_print > 10.0:
            print(f"local n={p.size} iter={iter} score={score:.6f} best={best_score:.6f}")
            last_print=now
```
But `score` and `best_score` are floats; accepting a worse score can increase `best_score` relation? best separate. Good.
After rejected and p mix, if score is not reset? We recompute.
Need ensure if b None (score inf) not.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
   seed = int(seed) if ...; budget = max(30, float(budget_s))
   rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
   start=time.monotonic(); deadline = start + budget - 30.0
   state = ...
   # If no time, fallback constant 64
   lengths_weights = ...
   # Add hlen
   hv = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
   if hv is not None:
      try: arr=np.asarray(hv,float); L=arr.size; if 16<=L<=16384: weight dict[L]=300
      except pass
   total_w=sum(weights.values())
   for n in sorted(weights):
       alloc = max(5.0, (budget - 30.0) * weights[n] / total_w)
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + alloc)
       search_length(n, end, rng, state)
       print(f"after n={n} global best={state['score']:.6f}")
   # leftover refine
   while time.monotonic() < deadline - 10.0 and state['seq'] is not None:
       base=state['seq'].copy()
       if rng.random()<0.4:
           base=perturb_normalized(base,rng,0.3)
       end=min(deadline-10.0, time.monotonic()+15.0)
       local_search(base,end,rng,state)
   # final exact check
   best=state['seq']
   if best is None:
      best=np.ones(64)
   best=safe_normalize(np.where(np.isfinite(best),best,0))
   # if exact evaluator available and size <= 8192 and time left>5
   if 'evaluate_sequence' in globals() and time.monotonic() < deadline + 10:
       sc=exact_evaluate(best)
       print exact
   out = best.tolist()
   # ensure nonnegative and finite
   result=[]
   for x in out:
      if not np.isfinite(x): x=0.0
      if x<0: x=0.0
      if x>1000: x=1000.0
      result.append(float(x))
   if not result: result=[1.0]
   return result
```

```

```python
import numpy as np
import time

"""
Algorithm summary:
  - The objective is scale-invariant, so every candidate is kept normalized to sum 1.
  - A fast FFT-based convolution is used for most evaluations; direct convolution
    is used for small n.
  - Several low-dimensional analytic initial profiles are generated (powers,
    beta-like, double-exponential, quadratic-exponential, low-frequency cosine
    mixtures, random iid/smooth arrays).
  - From the best initial profiles, a local search is run:
        * active-peak multiplicative gradient update (move mass away from
          partners that make the largest convolution coefficients),
        * random block multiplicative perturbations,
        * convex/geometric mixing with the current best.
  - The search is explored over several sequence lengths, with remaining time
    spent refining the global best sequence.
"""

DIRECT_N = 768
TARGET_SCORE_REF = 1.5027


def safe_normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.shape, 1.0 / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def next_power_two(x):
    n = 1
    while n < x:
        n <<= 1
    return n


def fast_convolution(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    req = 2 * n - 1
    if n <= DIRECT_N:
        b = np.convolve(p, p)
    else:
        N = next_power_two(req)
        fa = np.fft.fft(p, N)
        b = np.fft.ifft(fa * fa).real[:req]
    b = np.where(np.isfinite(b), b, 0.0)
    return np.maximum(b, 0.0)


def conv_score_b(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim != 1:
        p = p.reshape(-1)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf"), None
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return float("inf"), None
    p = p / s
    b = fast_convolution(p)
    if b.size == 0:
        return float("inf"), b
    m = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf"), b
    return float(2.0 * n * m), b


def candidate_score_add(cands, a):
    a = safe_normalize(a)
    sc, _ = conv_score_b(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
        cands.append((sc, a))


def item_score(x):
    return x[0]


def state_update(state, seq, score):
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if seq.size == 0:
        return
    if score < state["score"]:
        state["score"] = float(score)
        state["seq"] = seq.copy()
    by = state.get("by_n")
    if by is None:
        by = {}
        state["by_n"] = by
    n = int(seq.size)
    old = by.get(n)
    if old is None or score < old[0]:
        by[n] = [float(score), seq.copy()]


def seq_power(n, exponent, reverse=False, eps=1e-6):
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    a = np.power(t + eps, exponent)
    if reverse:
        a = a[::-1]
    return safe_normalize(a)


def seq_beta(n, p, q, eps=1e-6):
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    a = np.power(t + eps, p) * np.power(1.0 - t + eps, q)
    return safe_normalize(a)


def seq_double_exp(n, c0, c1, s):
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    a = np.exp(c0 * t) + s * np.exp(c1 * (1.0 - t))
    return safe_normalize(a)


def seq_exp_quad(n, A, B):
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    ex = A * t * t + B * t
    ex -= float(np.max(ex))
    a = np.exp(ex)
    return safe_normalize(a)


def seq_cos_smooth(n, rng, order=3, strength=0.9):
    if order <= 0:
        return safe_normalize(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    coeffs = rng.normal(0.0, 0.6, order)
    phases = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi, order)
    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(1, order + 1):
        a += strength * coeffs[k - 1] * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * k * t + phases[k - 1])
    a = np.clip(a, 1e-12, None)
    return safe_normalize(a)


def seq_iid(n, rng, kind=0):
    if kind == 0:
        a = rng.random(n)
    else:
        a = rng.exponential(1.0, n)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    return safe_normalize(a)


def generate_initials(n, rng):
    cands = []
    candidate_score_add(cands, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    h = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if h is not None:
        try:
            ha = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
            if ha.size == n:
                candidate_score_add(cands, ha)
        except Exception:
            pass

    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

    large = n > 8192
    if large:
        geom_R = [2.0, 3.0, 5.0, 10.0, 30.0, 100.0]
        beta_exp = [0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0]
        dep_c = [0.0, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0]
        dep_s = [0.0, 0.5, 1.0]
        n_smooth = 3
        n_iid = 2
    else:
        geom_R = [1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0, 8.0, 16.0, 32.0, 100.0]
        beta_exp = [-0.5, 0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0]
        dep_c = [0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0]
        dep_s = [0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0]
        n_smooth = 8
        n_iid = 5

    for R in geom_R:
        a = np.exp(np.log(R) * t)
        candidate_score_add(cands, a)
        candidate_score_add(cands, a[::-1])

    for pe in beta_exp:
        for qe in beta_exp:
            if pe == qe:
                continue
            a = np.power(t + 1e-6, pe) * np.power(1.0 - t + 1e-6, qe)
            candidate_score_add(cands, a)

    for c0 in dep_c:
        for c1 in dep_c:
            for s in dep_s:
                a = np.exp(c0 * t) + s * np.exp(c1 * (1.0 - t))
                candidate_score_add(cands, a)

    for _ in range(8):
        A = float(rng.uniform(-4.0, 12.0))
        B = float(rng.uniform(-5.0, 5.0))
        candidate_score_add(cands, seq_exp_quad(n, A, B))

    for _ in range(n_smooth):
        candidate_score_add(cands, seq_cos_smooth(n, rng, order=int(rng.integers(2, 5))))

    for _ in range(n_iid):
        kind = int(rng.integers(0, 2))
        candidate_score_add(cands, seq_iid(n, rng, kind))

    return cands


def peak_update(p, b, eta, top_count):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if b.size == 0:
        return p.copy()
    m = b.size
    top_count = max(1, int(top_count))
    if top_count >= m:
        idx = np.arange(m)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(b, m - top_count)[m - top_count:]
    vmax = float(np.max(b[idx]))
    if vmax <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(vmax):
        return p.copy()

    raw = b[idx] / vmax
    wk = np.maximum(0.0, raw - 0.70)
    if float(np.sum(wk)) <= 0.0:
        wk = raw
    if float(np.max(wk)) <= 0.0:
        return p.copy()
    wk = wk / float(np.max(wk))

    w = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for pos, rk in enumerate(idx):
        weight = float(wk[pos])
        if weight <= 0.0:
            continue
        k = int(rk)
        i0 = k - (n - 1)
        if i0 < 0:
            i0 = 0
        i1 = k
        if i1 > n - 1:
            i1 = n - 1
        if i0 > i1:
            continue
        j0 = k - i1
        j1 = k - i0
        w[i0:i1 + 1] += weight * p[j0:j1 + 1][::-1]

    wsum = float(np.sum(w))
    if wsum <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(wsum):
        return p.copy()
    w /= float(np.mean(w))

    alpha = 1e-6
    up = (1.0 - alpha) * np.exp(-eta * w) + alpha
    p2 = p * up
    p2 += 1e-12 * max(1.0, float(np.max(p)))
    p2 = np.where(np.isfinite(p2), p2, 0.0)
    p2 = np.maximum(p2, 0.0)
    s = float(p2.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return p.copy()
    return p2 / s


def perturb_normalized(p, rng, sigma=0.25):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p.copy()
    q = p.astype(np.float64, copy=True)
    nb = int(rng.integers(1, 3))
    for _ in range(nb):
        logw = float(rng.normal(-3.0, 1.2))
        width = int(np.exp(logw) * n)
        width = max(1, min(n, width))
        if width <= 0:
            width = 1
        if n > width:
            start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
        else:
            start = 0
        q[start:start + width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))

    if rng.random() < 0.15:
        width = int(min(n, max(1, n * float(rng.uniform(0.01, 0.20)))))
        if width <= 0:
            width = 1
        if n > width:
            start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
        else:
            start = 0
        q[start:start + width] *= 0.25

    q = np.where(np.isfinite(q), q, 0.0)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    q += 1e-12
    s = float(q.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return p.copy()
    return q / s


def mix_normalized(p1, p2, rng):
    p1 = np.asarray(p1, dtype=np.float64)
    p2 = np.asarray(p2, dtype=np.float64)
    if p1.size != p2.size or p1.size == 0:
        return p1.copy()
    if rng.random() < 0.5:
        q = np.sqrt(np.maximum(p1, 0.0) * np.maximum(p2, 0.0))
    else:
        w = float(rng.uniform(0.15, 0.85))
        q = w * p1 + (1.0 - w) * p2
    q = np.where(np.isfinite(q), q, 0.0)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    q += 1e-12
    s = float(q.sum(dtype=np.float64))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return p1.copy()
    return q / s


def local_search(parent, deadline, rng, state):
    parent = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64)
    if parent.size == 0:
        return
    p = safe_normalize(parent)
    score, b = conv_score_b(p)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return

    best = p.copy()
    best_score = float(score)
    state_update(state, best, best_score)

    start = time.monotonic()
    top_count = max(8, min(64, max(1, p.size // 16)))
    eta = 0.35
    temp = 1e-4
    rejected = 0
    it = 0
    last_print = start

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if not np.isfinite(score) or b is None:
            break

        if best_score < score * 0.98 and rejected > 100:
            p = best.copy()
            score = float(best_score)
            sc, b = conv_score_b(p)
            if not np.isfinite(sc):
                break

        r = float(rng.random())
        if r < 0.70:
            new = peak_update(p, b, eta, top_count)
        elif r < 0.85:
            new = perturb_normalized(p, rng, sigma=0.20)
        elif r < 0.95:
            new = mix_normalized(p, best, rng)
        else:
            new = perturb_normalized(p, rng, sigma=0.40)

        new_score, new_b = conv_score_b(new)
        it += 1
        if not np.isfinite(new_score):
            continue

        accepted = False
        if new_score < score:
            accepted = True
        else:
            ratio = new_score / max(score, 1e-300)
            delta = float(ratio - 1.0)
            prob = 0.0
            if 0.0 < delta < temp:
                if delta < 20.0 * temp:
                    prob = float(np.exp(-delta / max(temp, 1e-300)))
                else:
                    prob = 0.0
            if prob > 0.0 and rng.random() < prob:
                accepted = True

        if accepted:
            p = new.copy()
            b = new_b
            score = float(new_score)
            rejected = 0
            temp = max(1e-7, temp * 0.995)
        else:
            rejected += 1
            if rejected > 80:
                rejected = 0
                temp = max(1e-7, temp * 0.5)
                eta = max(0.05, eta * 0.75)
                p = 0.75 * p + 0.25 * best
                s = float(p.sum(dtype=np.float64))
                if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
                    p = best.copy()
                    score = float(best_score)
                    sc, b = conv_score_b(p)
                else:
                    p = p / s
                    score, b = conv_score_b(p)
                if not np.isfinite(score):
                    break

        if score < best_score:
            best = p.copy()
            best_score = float(score)
            state_update(state, best, best_score)

        now = time.monotonic()
        if now - last_print > 10.0:
            print(
                f"local n={p.size} iter={it} "
                f"score={score:.6f} local_best={best_score:.6f} "
                f"global_best={state['score']:.6f}"
            )
            last_print = now


def search_length(n, deadline, rng, state):
    start = time.monotonic()
    print(f"search_length n={n}, allocated until {deadline - start:.1f}s")
    cands = generate_initials(n, rng)
    if not cands:
        return

    cands.sort(key=item_score)
    for sc, seq in cands[:min(8, len(cands))]:
        state_update(state, seq, sc)

    if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
        return

    total = max(0.5, deadline - start)
    top = cands[:min(4, len(cands))]
    if len(top) == 0:
        return

    if len(top) == 1:
        ends = [deadline]
    elif len(top) == 2:
        ends = [start + 0.60 * total, deadline]
    elif len(top) == 3:
        ends = [start + 0.45 * total, start + 0.75 * total, deadline]
    else:
        ends = [start + 0.35 * total, start + 0.60 * total, start + 0.80 * total, deadline]

    for i, item in enumerate(top):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        seq = item[1]
        end = min(deadline, ends[i]) if i < len(ends) else deadline
        if end <= time.monotonic():
            continue
        local_search(seq, end, rng, state)

    print(f"search_length n={n} done global_best={state['score']:.6f}")


def exact_evaluate(seq):
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in seq])
        if np.isfinite(val):
            return float(val)
    except Exception:
        pass
    return float("inf")


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """Restartable multi-length search for low peak nonnegative step sequences."""
    try:
        seed = int(seed)
    except Exception:
        seed = 42
    try:
        budget = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    except Exception:
        budget = 2400.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    hard_deadline = start_time + budget - 30.0

    state = {"score": float("inf"), "seq": None, "by_n": {}}

    weights = {
        32: 15,
        64: 25,
        128: 50,
        256: 90,
        512: 150,
        1024: 220,
        2048: 300,
        4096: 340,
        8192: 340,
        16384: 250,
    }

    h = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if h is not None:
        try:
            ha = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
            L = int(ha.size)
            if 32 <= L <= 16384:
                weights[L] = weights.get(L, 0) + 300
        except Exception:
            pass

    total_w = max(1.0, float(sum(weights.values())))
    for n in sorted(weights):
        if time.monotonic() >= hard_deadline:
            break
        alloc = max(5.0, (budget - 30.0) * float(weights[n]) / total_w)
        end = min(hard_deadline, time.monotonic() + alloc)
        search_length(n, end, rng, state)

    # Refine the global best with the remaining budget.
    refine_until = hard_deadline - 10.0
    while time.monotonic() < refine_until:
        base = state["seq"]
        if base is None:
            break
        base = safe_normalize(base)
        if rng.random() < 0.35:
            base = perturb_normalized(base, rng, sigma=0.30)
        end = min(refine_until, time.monotonic() + 18.0)
        if end <= time.monotonic():
            break
        local_search(base, end, rng, state)

    best = state["seq"]
    if best is None or best.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(best)):
        best = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)

    best = safe_normalize(best)

    # Optional exact validation for modest lengths.
    if "evaluate_sequence" in globals() and best.size <= 8192 and time.monotonic() < hard_deadline + 5.0:
        ex = exact_evaluate(best)
        print(f"final exact check score={ex:.6f} len={best.size}")
    else:
        est, _ = conv_score_b(best)
        print(f"final approximate score={est:.6f} len={best.size}")

    out = []
    for x in best:
        y = float(x)
        if not np.isfinite(y):
            y = 0.0
        if y < 0.0:
            y = 0.0
        if y > 1000.0:
            y = 1000.0
        out.append(y)

    if not out:
        out = [1.0, 1.0, 1.0]

    return out
```
